# Data Structures and Algorithms (DSA): past exam questions

Every question from the photographed sheets, books and PDFs in `ex-question/` that belongs to this subject. The same question asked in several exams, or printed in several sources, is shown once. For each question you get **where it was asked** (the exams, with names normalised in `../exam-tags.json`) and **where it was taken from** (each source document, linked to its transcription and image).

- **Distinct questions:** 386 (from 446 records: Study note 1, Written 355, MCQ 75, Viva 15)
- **Exams it appears in:** 131
- **Corrections to printed answers:** 12
- **How counts work:** "Asked in (N)" counts different government/public-sector recruitment exams (BCS, NTRCA, BPSC, state banks, public bodies) and BUET M.Sc. admission tests. Private-bank exams and compiled viva experiences are listed but not counted. Coaching suggestion sheets and model tests never add to the count.

<a id="g0436"></a>

### Binary tree with nodes a..i has postorder a b f c h d e g i. Draw the tree and give its (… <sub>G0436</sub>

*Written*

Binary tree with nodes a..i has postorder a b f c h d e g i. Draw the tree and give its (approximate) array representation.

- **Asked in (1):** Petrobangla AM(CSE) 2024
- **Source:**
  - BOGMC (Petrobangla) Assistant Manager (2024), Q2(a) — [PDF-TEXT/BOGMC_PetroBangla_Assistant_Manager_2024_Solutions_HUO_Academy.md](../PDF-TEXT/BOGMC_PetroBangla_Assistant_Manager_2024_Solutions_HUO_Academy.md#p3)
- **Printed answer:** Solution says the tree cannot be built from postorder alone (root i; inorder needed).
- **Correction:** True in general, but the question asks for an array representation, which implies a complete binary tree. Under that assumption the tree is unique: root i; i->(h,g); h->(f,c); g->(d,e); f->(a,b); array (1-based) = [i, h, g, f, c, d, e, a, b]. The solution should give this.

Why do we use Tree data structures?

- **Asked in (0):** no dated exam tag on the source
- **Viva mentions:** 1
- **Source:**
  - Bank IT viva (compiled from candidates' viva experiences) (viva), Viva p13 Q1 (+p14 Q9) — [PDF-TEXT/Viva Questions Extracted from Viva Experiences.md](../PDF-TEXT/Viva%20Questions%20Extracted%20from%20Viva%20Experiences.md#p13); [PDF-TEXT/Viva Questions Extracted from Viva Experiences.md](../PDF-TEXT/Viva%20Questions%20Extracted%20from%20Viva%20Experiences.md#p14)

### Quicksort with the first element as pivot: analyse the time complexity when the array is … <sub>G0842</sub>

*Written*

Quicksort with the first element as pivot: analyse the time complexity when the array is already sorted.

- **Source:**
  - Petrobangla Assistant Manager (CSE) (BUET) (2024-05-31), Q2(b) — [BUET JOB/30_java-oop-sql-fsm-questions.md](../BUET%20JOB/30_java-oop-sql-fsm-questions.md) · [image](../BUET%20JOB/30_java-oop-sql-fsm-questions.jpg)
  - BOGMC (Petrobangla) Assistant Manager (2024), Q2(b) — [PDF-TEXT/BOGMC_PetroBangla_Assistant_Manager_2024_Solutions_HUO_Academy.md](../PDF-TEXT/BOGMC_PetroBangla_Assistant_Manager_2024_Solutions_HUO_Academy.md#p3)

- **Printed answer:** T(n) = T(n-1) + O(n) = n(n+1)/2 -> O(n^2).

<a id="g0430"></a>

### How does quick sort work? <sub>G0430</sub>

*Viva*

How does quick sort work?

- **Asked in (0):** no dated exam tag on the source
- **Viva mentions:** 1
- **Source:**
  - NTRCA 17th registration viva 2023 (2023) (viva), Q2 (candidate 2, VIVA-59) — [VIVA/08_ntrca-full-adder-and-osi.md](../VIVA/08_ntrca-full-adder-and-osi.md) · [image](../VIVA/08_ntrca-full-adder-and-osi.jpg)
- **Printed answer:** Divide and conquer: split the list into a part with smaller elements and a part with larger ones, recursively, until each part has one element
- **Correction:** Simplified: the split is made by partitioning around a chosen pivot.

## Linked list

<a id="g1277"></a>

### Differentiate between Linked list and Array? <sub>G1277</sub>

*Written*

Differentiate between Linked list and Array?

- **Asked in (5):** 17th NTRCA ICT (2023); BNPS AP 2017; BPSC AP 2021; BPSC AP 2023; BPSC ME 2021
- **Viva mentions:** 2
- **Source:**
  - NTRCA 17th registration viva 2023 (2023) (viva), Q7 — [VIVA/04_ntrca-keywords-and-dbms-keys.md](../VIVA/04_ntrca-keywords-and-dbms-keys.md) · [image](../VIVA/04_ntrca-keywords-and-dbms-keys.jpg)
  - Bank IT viva (compiled from candidates' viva experiences) (viva), Viva p13 Q4 — [PDF-TEXT/Viva Questions Extracted from Viva Experiences.md](../PDF-TEXT/Viva%20Questions%20Extracted%20from%20Viva%20Experiences.md#p13); [PDF-TEXT/Viva Questions Extracted from Viva Experiences.md](../PDF-TEXT/Viva%20Questions%20Extracted%20from%20Viva%20Experiences.md#p14)
  - Bit to Exabyte, Super ICT 150++ Questions Book (chapter-wise previous questions) — Data Structure, Q6 — [bit to exabyte/47_bte-g2-p33-data-structure-q01-14.md](../bit%20to%20exabyte/47_bte-g2-p33-data-structure-q01-14.md) · [image](../bit%20to%20exabyte/47_bte-g2-p33-data-structure-q01-14.jpg)
- **Printed answer:** Array: contiguous memory, fast access, costly insert/delete; linked list: nodes chained by links, easy insert/delete, slower access

### Array vs linked list. <sub>G3358</sub>

*Written*

Array vs linked list.

- **Asked in (1):** DUET AP 2025
- **Viva mentions:** 1
- **Source:**
  - Bank IT viva (compiled from candidates' viva experiences) (viva), Viva p13 Q3 — [PDF-TEXT/Viva Questions Extracted from Viva Experiences.md](../PDF-TEXT/Viva%20Questions%20Extracted%20from%20Viva%20Experiences.md#p13)

- **Printed answer:** Array: Stores elements in contiguous memory and supports fast random access; Linked List: Stores elements using nodes connected by pointers, allowing dynamic memory allocation

Write the advantages and disadvantages of using a linked list instead of an array.

- **Asked in (1):** 45th BCS CSE (2024)
- **Source:**
  - 45th BCS Written (CSE-971) (2024-01-31), Q21(b) — [BCS/09_bfs-dfs-and-linked-list.md](../BCS/09_bfs-dfs-and-linked-list.md) · [image](../BCS/09_bfs-dfs-and-linked-list.jpg)

- **Printed answer:** Linked list: only a pointer changes. Queue/stack need repeated pops and pushes, and a heap must re-heapify.

<a id="g0570"></a>

### Array vs Linked List — which one is better? <sub>G0570</sub>

*Viva*

Array vs Linked List — which one is better?

- **Asked in (0):** no dated exam tag on the source
- **Viva mentions:** 1
- **Source:**
  - Bank IT viva (compiled from candidates' viva experiences) (viva), Viva p13 Q2 — [PDF-TEXT/Viva Questions Extracted from Viva Experiences.md](../PDF-TEXT/Viva%20Questions%20Extracted%20from%20Viva%20Experiences.md#p13)
- **Printed answer:** Neither is always better; it depends on the application. Arrays are faster for indexing, while Linked Lists are better for frequent insertions and deletions

<a id="g0572"></a>

### Can Divide and Conquer be applied to Linked Lists? <sub>G0572</sub>

*Viva*

Can Divide and Conquer be applied to Linked Lists?

- **Asked in (0):** no dated exam tag on the source
- **Viva mentions:** 1
- **Source:**
  - Bank IT viva (compiled from candidates' viva experiences) (viva), Viva p14 Q6 — [PDF-TEXT/Viva Questions Extracted from Viva Experiences.md](../PDF-TEXT/Viva%20Questions%20Extracted%20from%20Viva%20Experiences.md#p14)
- **Printed answer:** Yes, but not efficiently for searching because linked lists don't support direct access. It is commonly used for algorithms like Merge Sort on linked lists

A circular queue has 6 locations (0-5). Starting empty: add A, C, D; insert F; delete two; add K, L, M; delete two; add R. Give FRONT and REAR after each step.

- **Asked in (1):** 38th BCS (2018)
- **Source:**
  - 38th BCS Written (2018), Q7(a) — [BCS/27_oop-advantages-data-structures-circular-queue.md](../BCS/27_oop-advantages-data-structures-circular-queue.md) · [image](../BCS/27_oop-advantages-data-structures-circular-queue.jpg); [BCS/29_circular-queue-solution-operating-system-deadlock.md](../BCS/29_circular-queue-solution-operating-system-deadlock.md) · [image](../BCS/29_circular-queue-solution-operating-system-deadlock.jpg)
- **Printed answer:** Steps: (-1,-1) → F=0,R=2 → R=3 → F=2 → R=0 (M wraps to 0) → F=4 → R=1. Final FRONT = 4, REAR = 1.

<a id="g0573"></a>

### Difference between Stack and Queue. <sub>G0573</sub>

*Viva*

Difference between Stack and Queue.

- **Asked in (0):** no dated exam tag on the source
- **Viva mentions:** 1
- **Source:**
  - Bank IT viva (compiled from candidates' viva experiences) (viva), Viva p14 Q7 — [PDF-TEXT/Viva Questions Extracted from Viva Experiences.md](../PDF-TEXT/Viva%20Questions%20Extracted%20from%20Viva%20Experiences.md#p14)
- **Printed answer:** Stack: Follows LIFO (Last In, First Out); insertion and deletion occur at the same end; Queue: Follows FIFO (First In, First Out); insertion occurs at the rear and deletion at the front

<a id="g0576"></a>

### What is Stack? <sub>G0576</sub>

*Viva*

What is Stack?

- **Asked in (0):** no dated exam tag on the source
- **Viva mentions:** 1
- **Source:**
  - Bank IT viva (compiled from candidates' viva experiences) (viva), Viva p14 Q12 — [PDF-TEXT/Viva Questions Extracted from Viva Experiences.md](../PDF-TEXT/Viva%20Questions%20Extracted%20from%20Viva%20Experiences.md#p14)
- **Printed answer:** A Stack is a linear data structure that follows the LIFO (Last In, First Out) principle. Common operations are Push (insert) and Pop (remove)

<a id="g0577"></a>

### What is Queue? <sub>G0577</sub>

*Viva*

What is Queue?

- **Asked in (0):** no dated exam tag on the source
- **Viva mentions:** 1
- **Source:**
  - Bank IT viva (compiled from candidates' viva experiences) (viva), Viva p15 Q13 — [PDF-TEXT/Viva Questions Extracted from Viva Experiences.md](../PDF-TEXT/Viva%20Questions%20Extracted%20from%20Viva%20Experiences.md#p15)
- **Printed answer:** A Queue is a linear data structure that follows the FIFO (First In, First Out) principle. Elements are enqueued at the rear and dequeued from the front

Differentiate divide-and-conquer and dynamic programming.

- **Asked in (2):** BCC AP 2021; WZPGCL AE(CSE) 2023
- **Source:**
  - BCC Assistant Programmer (BUET) (2021-02-12), Q3 — [BUET JOB/07_subnet-grep-divide-conquer.md](../BUET%20JOB/07_subnet-grep-divide-conquer.md) · [image](../BUET%20JOB/07_subnet-grep-divide-conquer.jpg)

- **Printed answer:** Table: D&C has independent subproblems, top-down, recursive (merge sort, binary search); DP has overlapping subproblems solved once and stored, bottom-up (matrix-chain multiplication).

<a id="g0160"></a>

### n customers request time slots [start, end]; overlapping requests cannot both be served. … <sub>G0160</sub>

*Written*

n customers request time slots [start, end]; overlapping requests cannot both be served. Write an algorithm to serve the maximum number of customers.

- **Asked in (1):** BPDB AE(CSE) 2023
- **Source:**
  - BPDB Assistant Engineer (CSE) (BUET) (2023-02-24), Q12 — [BUET JOB/15_bpdb-customer-slot-scheduling.md](../BUET%20JOB/15_bpdb-customer-slot-scheduling.md) · [image](../BUET%20JOB/15_bpdb-customer-slot-scheduling.jpg); [BUET JOB/16_inclusion-exclusion-and-equilibrium.md](../BUET%20JOB/16_inclusion-exclusion-and-equilibrium.md) · [image](../BUET%20JOB/16_inclusion-exclusion-and-equilibrium.jpg); [BUET JOB/31_bpdb-protocols-sql-algorithms.md](../BUET%20JOB/31_bpdb-protocols-sql-algorithms.md) · [image](../BUET%20JOB/31_bpdb-protocols-sql-algorithms.jpg)
- **Printed answer:** Greedy: sort by start time, add each slot that does not overlap the schedule, return schedule length (Python).
- **Correction:** Sorting by start time is not optimal (e.g. [1,10],[2,3],[4,5] gives 1 instead of 2). Sort by end time and take each slot whose start ≥ last chosen end, O(n log n).

<a id="g0015"></a>

### What are the differences between BFS and DFS? For what sort of problems is it better to u… <sub>G0015</sub>

*Written*

What are the differences between BFS and DFS? For what sort of problems is it better to use BFS than DFS?

- **Asked in (1):** 45th BCS CSE (2024)
- **Source:**
  - 45th BCS Written (CSE-971) (2024-01-31), Q21(a) — [BCS/09_bfs-dfs-and-linked-list.md](../BCS/09_bfs-dfs-and-linked-list.md) · [image](../BCS/09_bfs-dfs-and-linked-list.jpg)
- **Printed answer:** BFS uses a queue and finds shortest paths near the source; DFS uses a stack and backtracking with less memory; both O(V+E).
- **Correction:** Minor errors: the DFS column says the stack is used for 'BFS traversal' (it should say DFS), and 'BFS is slower than DFS' is not generally true, since both are O(V+E). The 'when to use BFS' part should name shortest paths in unweighted graphs and solutions close to the root.

<a id="g0176"></a>

### Build a heap from {15, 19, 10, 7, 17, 16} and redraw it after deleting element 7. <sub>G0176</sub>

*Written*

Build a heap from {15, 19, 10, 7, 17, 16} and redraw it after deleting element 7.

- **Asked in (1):** PGCB AE(CSE) 2021
- **Source:**
  - PGCB Assistant Engineer (CSE) (BUET) (2021-09-30), Q7 — [BUET JOB/04_heap-insert-and-delete-7.md](../BUET%20JOB/04_heap-insert-and-delete-7.md) · [image](../BUET%20JOB/04_heap-insert-and-delete-7.jpg)
- **Printed answer:** Draws a binary search tree (15 root; 10, 19; 7; 17; 16) and deletes leaf 7.
- **Correction:** The tree drawn is a BST, not a heap. Max-heap by insertion: 19 / 17,16 / 7,15,10; deleting 7 (move last element 10 into its place) gives 19 / 17,16 / 10,15. (Min-heap: 7 / 10,15 / 19,17,16; deleting root 7 gives 10 / 16,15 / 19,17.)

<a id="g0832"></a>

### Write the recurrence equation of binary search and solve it. <sub>G0832</sub>

*Written*

Write the recurrence equation of binary search and solve it.

- **Asked in (1):** Petrobangla AM(IT) 2022
- **Source:**
  - Petrobangla Assistant Manager (IT) (BUET) (2022-09-16), Q7 — [PETROBANGLA/15_adc-and-binary-search-recurrence.md](../PETROBANGLA/15_adc-and-binary-search-recurrence.md) · [image](../PETROBANGLA/15_adc-and-binary-search-recurrence.jpg)
- **Printed answer:** States T(n) = T(n/2) + 1, then guesses O(n log n) and 'proves' it using T(n) = 2T(n/2) + n
- **Correction:** Solves the merge-sort recurrence instead. T(n) = T(n/2) + c gives T(n) = c·log2 n + T(1) = O(log n).

<a id="g0571"></a>

### Why is Binary Search faster than Sequential Search? <sub>G0571</sub>

*Viva*

Why is Binary Search faster than Sequential Search?

- **Asked in (0):** no dated exam tag on the source
- **Viva mentions:** 1
- **Source:**
  - Bank IT viva (compiled from candidates' viva experiences) (viva), Viva p14 Q5 — [PDF-TEXT/Viva Questions Extracted from Viva Experiences.md](../PDF-TEXT/Viva%20Questions%20Extracted%20from%20Viva%20Experiences.md#p14)
- **Printed answer:** Binary Search repeatedly divides the search space in half, giving O(log n) time complexity. Sequential Search checks elements one by one, taking O(n) time

## Arrays

<a id="g0152"></a>

### Find the maximum-sum contiguous subarray of A = [-2, 1, -3, -1, 2, 1, -5, 4] (the questio… <sub>G0152</sub>

*Written*

Find the maximum-sum contiguous subarray of A = [-2, 1, -3, -1, 2, 1, -5, 4] (the question-only sheet prints A[3] = 1).

- **Asked in (1):** BPDB AE(CSE) 2023
- **Source:**
  - BPDB Assistant Engineer (CSE) (BUET) (2023-02-24), Q4 — [BUET JOB/21_linux-count-and-max-subarray.md](../BUET%20JOB/21_linux-count-and-max-subarray.md) · [image](../BUET%20JOB/21_linux-count-and-max-subarray.jpg); [BUET MSC/02_bpdb-assistant-engineer-questions-1-9.md](../BUET%20MSC/02_bpdb-assistant-engineer-questions-1-9.md) · [image](../BUET%20MSC/02_bpdb-assistant-engineer-questions-1-9.jpg)
- **Printed answer:** Kadane's algorithm; claims max sum 6 with subarray [1, -3, -1, 2, 1, -5, 4].
- **Correction:** Wrong (copied from the classic array [-2,1,-3,4,-1,2,1,-5,4]); the stated subarray sums to -1. For the given array the maximum sum is 4 (subarray [4]; also [1,2,1] if A[3]=1).
